Subtyping in DHOL – Extended preprint
Colin Rothgang, Florian Rabe

Abstract
The recently introduced dependent typed higher-order logic (DHOL) offers an interesting compromise between expressiveness and automation support. It sacrifices the decidability of its type system in order to significantly extend its expressiveness over standard HOL. Yet it retains strong automated theorem proving support via a sound and complete translation to HOL. We leverage this design to extend DHOL with refinement and quotient types. Both of these are commonly requested by practitioners but rarely provided by automated theorem provers. This is because they inherently require undecidable typing and thus are very difficult to retrofit to decidable type systems. But with DHOL already doing the heavy lifting, adding them is not only possible but elegant and simple. Concretely, we add refinement and quotient types as special cases of subtyping. This turns the associated canonical inclusion resp. projection maps into identity maps and thus avoids costly changes in representation. We present the syntax, semantics, and translation to HOL for the extended language, including the proofs of soundness and completeness.



1
Introduction and Related Work

Motivation

Recently dependently typed higher-order logic (DHOL) was introduced
[
21
]
.
It is a variant of HOL
[
5
,
2
]
that uses dependent function types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
instead of simple function types
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
It is designed to remain as simple and as close to HOL and ATPs as possible while meeting the frequent user demand of dependent types.
Notably, contrary to typical formulations of dependent type theory, DHOL features a straightforward equality and classical Booleans at the cost of making typing undecidable.

Concretely, DHOL uses a type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
of propositions in the style of HOL, and equality
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
of typed terms is a proposition, whose truth may depend on axioms in the theory or assumptions in the context.
Equality
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
of types (which is not a proposition but a meta-level judgment) uses a straightforward congruence rule: if a dependent type constructor is applied to equal arguments, it produces equal types.
Thus, equality of types and typing are undecidable.
To yield practical tool support, DHOL reduces typing judgments to a series of proof obligations, and
[
21
]
gives a sound and complete translation to HOL that allows using existing automated theorem provers for HOL to discharge these.

While undecidable typing is not used by most current ATPs or ITPs, it is justified by the pragmatic consideration that the ultimate task of theorem proving is undecidable anyway, and the difficulty of typing-related proof obligations is often small in comparison.
Follow-up work on DHOL includes a native ATP for DHOL
[
16
]
and extensions with a choice operator
[
19
]
and polymorphism
[
20
]
.

Contribution

The present paper leverages this key design choice and extends DHOL’s expressivity at low cost by adding refinement and quotient types.
Both work elegantly in languages with undecidable typing so that DHOL, for which the necessary meta-theory and infrastructure already exist, is a good base to support them.
Indeed, the necessary changes to DHOL’s syntax and semantics turned out to be few and simple — only the extension of the proof was difficult.
We see DHOL as an intermediate between the automation support of HOL and the more expressive type theories of interactive provers such as those based on richer dependent type theories.
In this sense, the present paper pushes the boundary of ATP-near languages a little further.

Refinement types

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt
is the subtype of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
consisting of the terms satisfying the predicate
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
.
They correspond to comprehension in set theory.
They were already proposed in
[
21
]
(with ad-hoc subtyping rules), and here we give a systematic treatment.
Critically, our refinement types avoid a change in representation: the injection
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
is always a no-op, i.e., an identity map.
This is in contrast to encodings of refinement types in decidable type theories, such as using the type
\Sigma{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\,x
, or in set-theory, where, e.g., the injection
(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})\to(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})
is not a no-op.

Quotient types

\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
, intuitively, consist of all equivalence classes of the equivalence relation
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
.
Again we avoid representation changes:
We use all terms of type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
as terms of type
\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
and adjust the equality
\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,
to obtain the quotient semantics.
Thus, the projection
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
is a no-op and we have the subtyping relation
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
.
In contrast, the usual definitions in set theory (via equivalence classes) or in decidable type theories (via setoids) require explicit changes in representation.

The statement
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
may look odd.
It is sound because we use a different equality relation at the two types:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
but not the other way round.
This approach captures the mathematical practice of using elements of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
as elements of the quotient, often to the point that readers do not even notice anymore they are technically working with equivalence classes.

Together, this yields a subtype hierarchy of refinements and quotients of
A
:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{false}}}\hskip 1.13809pt\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\!\ldots\!\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\!\ldots\!\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}}\hskip 1.13809pt\!\mathbin{\equiv}\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\!\mathbin{\equiv}\!\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,$}}}}\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\!\ldots\!\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\!\ldots\!\!\mathbin{\prec:}\!\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}$}}}}

from initial (empty) type to terminal (singleton) type.

Related Work

Refinement types have been considered on top of HOl in
[
14
]
, but that logic has no subtyping, unlike our extension of DHOL.
Definitional quotient types have been considered in HOL as well in
[
11
]
or
[
12
]
, but in those logics the quotients are completely new types leading to explicit changes of representations. Even their combination has been considered before in HOL
[
18
]
, using PERs to represent types, similarly to our own approach, but again with an explicit change of representation rather than subtyping as in our approach for DHOL.

In formal systems, the approach of quotients as supertypes has been adopted only occasionally, e.g., in NuPRL’s quotients
[
6
]
or in Quotient Haskell
[
3
]
.
NuPRL’s type theory in particular features refinement and quotient types similar to ours and uses essentially the same PER semantics
[
1
]
.
The main difference to our approach is that DHOL tries to be as close to HOL (and thus HOL ATPs) as possible whereas NuPRL uses a very rich type theory.

PVS
[
17
]
subsumes DHOL and refinement types with polymorphism.
It does not support quotients, but its record subtyping resembles our quotient subtyping.
Notably, it treats refinement subtyping and record subtyping as two separate judgments with slightly different rules.

In soft type systems, all types are refinements of a fixed universe of objects.
For example, Mizar’s
[
4
]
type system is inherently undecidable and supports dependent types and refinement types.
It supports quotient types but only with a change in representation and not as supertypes.

Most ITPs allow users to construct refinement and quotient types at the cost of representation changes.
Systems based on decidable dependent type theory like Coq
[
7
]
or Lean
[
8
]
typically use
\Sigma
-types for refinement and setoids for quotients.
Lean provides some kernel support for quotients.
Systems based on HOL
[
10
]
use the subtype definition principle to introduce types isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt
.

[
20
]
adds polymorphism to DHOL, and in future work we want to combine both features.
The key challenge will be to support
subtype-bounded
polymorphism.

Overview

We give a self-contained definition of DHOL in Sect.
2
.
Then we add subtyping in Sect.
3
, refinements in Sect.
4
, and quotients in Sect.
5
.
We develop the meta-theory in Sect.
6
(type normalization) and Sect.
7
(soundness/completeness).
We present an application to typed set theory in Sect.
8
.

2
Preliminaries: Dependently Typed Higher-Order Logic

The DHOL
[
21
]

grammar
uses
terms
and
types
.
A theory
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}
declares typed constants
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{c}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
, axioms
\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
, and dependent type symbols
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}A_{1}}}.~\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}A_{n}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}
, which are applied to terms to obtain base types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}_{1}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}_{n}
.
Contexts declare typed variables
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
and local assumptions
\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
(but no new types).
Dependent functions
\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
(written
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
does not occur free in
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
) map terms
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
to terms of type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})
.
We recover HOL as the fragment in which all base types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}
have arity
0
, in which case all function types are simple.

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \bbc\quad}l@{\quad}l}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}\hfil\ ::=&\circ\;|\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T},\,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\mathord{:}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!)^{*}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{c}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T},\,\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}&\text{theories}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\hfil\ ::=&.\;|\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}&\text{contexts}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\hfil\ ::=&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}^{*}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}&\text{types}\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}\hfil\ ::=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{c}}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\;|\;\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\;|\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\;|\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\;|\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}&\text{terms (incl. propositions)}\end{array}

Following typical HOL-style
[
2
]
, DHOL defines all connectives and quantifiers from the equality connective
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
.
For example, forall is defined as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}:=\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}.

In particular, we can define the usual connectives and quantifiers and derive their usual proof rules.
The only subtlety is that, DHOL needs
dependent
binary connectives: in an implication
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}
, the well-formedness of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}
may depend on the truth of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
, and accordingly for conjunction
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\land\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}
which is defined as
\neg(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\Rightarrow\neg\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G})
.
Because we see no way to define these from equality, we make dependent implication an additional primitive.

DHOL uses axiomatic equality
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
in the style of FOL and HOL with a straightforward congruence rule for base types: type equality
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}_{1}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}_{n}\mathbin{\equiv}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}_{1}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}_{n}
holds if each
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}_{i}
is equal to
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}_{i}
.
This makes type equality and thus typing undecidable.
In line with HOL’s simplicity and unlike dependent type theories based on Martin-Löf type theory
[
15
]
, there is no support for type-valued computation like large elimination.

Example 1
(Lists)

As an accessible running example, we show a formalization of lists over some type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}
, both plain lists
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}
and lists
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}
with fixed length.
Notably, the well-typedness of the statement of associativity of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{lconc}}
now requires the associativity of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{plus}}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{zero}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{succ}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{plus}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}},\\
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{nil}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{cons}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}},\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{lnil}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{zero}},\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{lcons}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{succ}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}),\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{lconc}}\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m,n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{plus}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n})\end{array}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s})\ 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\,\text{}

Rules for validity: lookup, implication, Boolean equality and Boolean extensionality

\cfrac{\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{F}\text{ in }{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\ \mathsf{Ctx}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\;\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F^{\prime}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\,\text{}

\cfrac{\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\text{ in }{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\ \mathsf{Ctx}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{G}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{false}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\,\text{}

Figure 2:
DHOL Rules

DHOL uses the
judgments
given in Fig.
1
and the
rules
listed in Fig.
2
.
Note that equality of terms is a special case of validity, whereas equality of types is not a Boolean but a separate judgment.
Thus, users cannot state axioms equating types, and type equality is defined only by congruence.
The rules are straightforward.
In particular, type equality is checked structurally and reduced to a set of term equalities, which must then be discharged by an ATP.

Furthermore many well-known admissible HOL rules are also admissible in DHOL, see Appendix
0.A.2
.

satisfying
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Figure 3:
Definition of the Translation DHOL
\to
HOL

The
semantics
for DHOL and a
practical ATP workflow
are given by a sound and complete translation to HOL.
The translation is dependency erasure, e.g., translating dependent types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{1}}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{n}}
to simple types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}
, effectively “‘merging”’ all instances of a dependent type into a large simple type.
Fig.
3
shows the details.

Typing and equality at
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
are recovered by generating a partial equivalence relation (PER)
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}
for every HOL-type
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
A PER is a symmetric-transitive relation and the same as an equivalence relation on a subtype of
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Thus,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
corresponds in HOL to the quotient of the appropriate subtype of
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}
.

DHOL terms are translated to their HOL analogues except that equality is translated to the respective PER:
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
In particular, the predicate
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
captures whether
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
is a term of type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
.
For
n
-ary type symbols
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}
, the translation generates an
n+2
-ary predicate
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{a}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{a}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{1}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{n}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is the PER for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{1}}}\ \ldots\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t_{n}}}
.
For function types, the PER is the usual condition for logical relations: functions are related if they map related inputs to related outputs.

3
Subtyping

The treatment of quotients as supertypes and the use of different equality relations at different types are subtly difficult.
Thus, we first introduce subtyping by its defining extensional property, from which we will derive all subtyping rules:

Definition 1
(Subtyping)

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
abbreviates
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.

Lemma 1

In any extension of DHOL,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
iff
\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,
is derivable.

Proof

Left-to-right: We construct the function
(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
and derive the desired rule using the typing rule for function application.

Right-to-left: We start with
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
and apply the derivable rule.

This subtyping relation prevents
incidental
subtype instances, for which the rule from Lem.
1
is admissible but not derivable.
For example,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{false}}}\hskip 1.13809pt
is a subtype of all refinements of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
, but not of all types.
More generally, this definition precludes using induction on the terms of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
to conclude
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
This restriction ensures that subtyping is preserved under, e.g., theory extensions, substitution, or language extensions.
Importantly, subtyping preserves equality:

Lemma 2

Consider some extension of DHOL with productions and rules. Assume (
\ast
) that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
implies
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
Let
F:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
iff
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}F:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
; and if these hold, then also
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}F
.

(
\ast
) is a very mild assumption and satisfied by all extensions given in this paper.

Proof

Left-to-right: The assumption yields
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
We get
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}

(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
from congruence of function application and reflexivity and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
by
\beta
-reduction.

Right-to-left: Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
. Instantiating
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
twice with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and applying modus ponens with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
yields
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, from which we get
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.

Lemma 3
(Preorder of Types)

In any extension of DHOL, subtyping is reflexive (in the sense that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
implies
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
) and transitive.

Proof

Reflexivity: The assumption yields
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
Applying both to a term
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
of type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
and
\beta
-reducing yields the rule from Lem.
1
.
Transitivity follows immediately from Lem.
1
.

We also want to make subtyping an order.
Anti-symmetry with respect to
\mathbin{\equiv}
is not derivable directly, i.e., we might have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
, in which case
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
would have the same terms, without being equal.
Therefore, we
add
the anti-symmetry rule

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hypertarget{STantisym}{}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\text{STantisym}

Notably, this is the only
change
made to DHOL so far — everything before has just been abbreviations.
This change is conservative in the following sense:

Theorem 3.1
(Conservativity)

For DHOL as defined so far (without the extension we introduce below), we have

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
iff
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.

Proof

We show by induction on derivations that each term has a unique type up to type equality and that all term equality axioms preserve typing.

Theorem 3.2
(Variance and Congruence for Function Types)

The usual rules for function types are derivable:

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B^{\prime}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\prec:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B^{\prime}}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B^{\prime}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\equiv}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B^{\prime}}}\,\text{}

The second rule is primitive in DHOL but derivable in DHOL with subtyping.

Proof

The first rule follows from the definition of subtyping and
\eta
-expansion.
The second rule is derived by (
), establishing the hypotheses using the variance rule and reflexivity of subtyping.

4
Refinement types

To add refinement types, we add only one production for types.
We do not add productions for terms — refinement types only provide new typing properties for the existing terms.
Then we add rules for, respectively, formation, introduction, elimination (two rules), and equality:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \bbc\quad}l@{\quad}l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hfil\ ::=&\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}&\text{type $\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$ refined by predicate $\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}$ on $\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\,\text{}\\[11.38109pt]
\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\,\text{}\end{array}

Example 2
(Refining Lists by Length)

We extend Ex.
1
by defining fixed-length lists as a refinement of lists.
First, we axiomatize a predicate
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}
on lists:

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt\triangleright\,\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{nil}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{zero}}\\
\triangleright\,\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{cons}}\ x\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{succ}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l})\end{array}

Then we define
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ n\;:=\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{length}}\ l\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\hskip 1.13809pt
, and we can derive

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{nil}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{zero}}\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{nat}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{cons}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{llist}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{succ}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n})

Theorem 4.1
(Congruence and Variance)

The following rules are derivable if the involved types are well-formed:

\begin{array}[]{l}\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\;\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\,\text{}\\[10.00002pt]
\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,\text{}\end{array}

Proof

To derive the first rule, we assume the hypotheses and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt
.
The elimination rules yield
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, then the hypotheses yield
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}
resp.
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, finally the introduction rule yields
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}}\hskip 1.13809pt
.

To derive the second rule, we apply (
) and use the introduction/elimination rules to show the two subtype relationships.

These then imply the other rules.

5
Quotient types

To add quotient types we also extend the grammar with only one production for the type and rules for formation, introduction, elimination, and equality, where
\mathrm{EqRel}(r)
abbreviates that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}
is an equivalence relation:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \bbc\quad}l@{\quad}l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hfil\ ::=&\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}&\text{quotient of $\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$ by equivalence relation $\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}\end{array}

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\mathrm{EqRel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r})}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\text{}

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s},\,\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}}]}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}}}]\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}}}]}\,\text{}

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\mathrm{EqRel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r})}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t})}\,\text{}

Example 3
(Sets)

We extend Ex.
1
by obtaining sets as a quotient of lists.
First, we axiomatize a predicate for containing an element:

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt\triangleright\,\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\neg(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{nil}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\\
\triangleright\,\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}}.~(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{cons}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l})\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\vee\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\end{array}

Now we can define
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}:=\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}l}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}$}}}}
as the type of lists containing the same elements.
The equality at
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
immediately yields extensionality
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\Leftrightarrow\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{contains}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}z}\right)
.

Any
l\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}
can be used as a representative of the respective equivalence class in
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
, and operations on sets can be defined via operations on lists, e.g., we can establish
\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
.
To derive this, we assume
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
and apply the elimination rule twice.
First we apply it with
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}
;
we have to show
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
under the assumption that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
are equal as sets.
That yields a term
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
.
We assume
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{v}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
and apply the elimination rule again with
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
to obtain
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{conc}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{v}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
, and then conclude via
\lambda
-abstraction and
\eta
-reduction.

The elimination rule above looks overly complex.
It can be understood best by comparing it to the following, simpler and more intuitive rule

\displaystyle\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\,\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}}}]}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\text{}(\ast)

This rule captures the well-known condition that a function
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
on
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
may be used as a function on
\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
if
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
maps equivalent representatives
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
equally.
It follows from our elimination rule by putting
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, but is subtly weaker:

Example 4

Continuing Ex.
3
, assume a total order on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}
and a function
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{list}}|_{\mathrm{nonEmpty}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}
picking the maximum from a non-empty list.
We should be able to apply
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}
to some
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
that we know to be non-empty.
But if we try to apply
(\ast)
to obtain
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathord{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}
, we get stuck trying to prove
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
that are representatives of an
arbitrary
equivalence class of lists.
We cannot use the condition that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}
is non-empty and thus only non-empty lists need to be considered.
Thus, we cannot derive the well-formedness of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.

Our elimination rule remedies that: here we need to show
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{\mathtt{obj}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
that are representatives of
the class of

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}
.
Thus, we can use that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}
are non-empty and that thus
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
is well-formed.

In dependent type theory, the two elimination rules are equivalent because we have a type
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and can use
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle A$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle A$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle A$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle r$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle r$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle r$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle r$}}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
as the return type.
This is not possible in DHOL where
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
is not a type but a Boolean.

Theorem 5.1
(Congruence and Variance)

The following rules are derivable if the involved types are well-formed and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}
are equivalence relations:

\begin{array}[]{l}\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\;\triangleright\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}}}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}}}}\,\text{}\\[10.00002pt]
\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\text{}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{\equiv}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}}}}\,\text{}\end{array}

Proof

For the first rule: Assume the hypotheses and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
.
Apply the elimination rule with
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}=\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\;\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s},\;\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}}

by the equality rule (using
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A^{\prime}}
and the second assumption). For the second rule, apply (
) and use the introduction/elimination rules to show the two subtype relationships.
The other rules follow from those two.

6
Normalizing Types

To build a type-checker, we derive normalization rules that reduce subtyping conditions to validity conditions that can then be discharged via an ATP.
We prove rules for merging consecutive refinements and quotients and for the
4
possible combinations of a function type with a refinement or quotient:

Theorem 6.1
(Repeated Refinement/Quotient)

The following are derivable whenever the LHS is well-formed

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}}\hskip 1.13809pt\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\wedge\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\hskip 1.13809pt

(RR)
