\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}})$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}$}}}}\mathbin{\equiv}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}$}}}}

(QQ)

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}})|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\mathbin{\equiv}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}

(RQ)

Proof

(
RR
)
:
well-formedness of the LHS yields
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
, so
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
is well-formed as
\wedge
is a
dependent
conjunction and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
can be assumed while checking
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.
The well-formedness of the right-hand side (RHS) follows.
Verifying the equality is straightforward by showing subtyping in both directions.

(
QQ
)
:
well-formedness of the LHS yields
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\to\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
, so
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
implies
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}
(and thus the RHS) is well-formed.
The relation on the RHS is an equivalence relation since
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}
is.
To verify the type equality, we use Lem.
2
and show that both types induce the same equality.
In particular, the type of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r^{\prime}}
already guarantees that it subsumes
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}
.

(
RQ
)
: well-formedness of the LHS yields
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
, implying
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
. The well-formedness of the RHS follows. (Note the other direction does not hold in general.)
To show the equality, we show both subtyping directions.
For LHS
\mathbin{\prec:}
RHS, we assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and apply the elimination rule for quotients using
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}=\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
.
(Critically, this step would not go through if we had only used the weaker rule
\ast
in Sect.
5
.)
For RHS
\mathbin{\prec:}
LHS, we assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
and apply the elimination rule for quotients using
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.

Theorem 6.2
(Refinement/Quotient in a Function Type)

The following are derivable if either side is well-formed:

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)\mathbin{\equiv}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}).~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)}\hskip 1.13809pt

(RCod)

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\equiv}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\Rightarrow\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\right)}\hskip 1.13809pt

(QDom)

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{:\succ}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}}}

(QCod)

The following is derivable if the RHS is well-formed:

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{:\succ}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\Rightarrow\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\!\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\Rightarrow\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\!\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\Rightarrow\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\!\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\!\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\!\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\Rightarrow\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\,\!\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}}}\!\!\!\!

(RDom)

Proof

(
RCod
)
:
Both subtyping directions are straightforward, as terms on either side are given by
\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
where
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
has type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
and satisfies
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}
.

(
QDom
)
:
Both subtyping directions are straightforward, as both sides are subtypes of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
so their elements must preserve
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}
.

(
QCod
)
:
Assume a term
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
of RHS-type and show
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
using the rules for quotients.

(
RDom
)
:
Assume a term
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
of RHS-type and show
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
using the quotient elimination rule.
The well-formedness of the LHS does not imply the well-formedness of the RHS since the well-formedness of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
can rely on
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.

Maybe surprisingly, two of the subtyping laws in Thm.
6.2
are not equalities.
The law for the refined domain
must not
be an equality:

Example 5
(Refined Domain (
RDom
))

The assumption
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
makes more terms well-typed, thus there may be functions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
that are not a restriction of a function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
Consider the theory
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{c}}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{false}}
is empty and so are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and its quotients.
But with
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}=\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{c}}\;\mathord{:}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
.

The law for the quotiented codomain
may or may not
be an equality.
This is related to the axiom of choice.
Consider the two statements

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\exists\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}repr}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{repr}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\to\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\exists\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}

(Note that the first one is well-typed because
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
.) Both have a claim to be called the axiom of choice: The first one expresses that every equivalence relation has a system of representatives.
The second generalizes this to a family of equivalence relations.
The latter implies the former (put
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}:=\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}:=\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
).
In the simply-typed case the former also implies the latter (pick
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{repr}\circ\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
for
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}
); but in the dependently-typed case,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}
may depend on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}
and the implication might not hold.

Both statements construct a new term from an existing one (
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{repr}
behaves like the identity, and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}
like
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
) that has a different type but behaves the same up to quotienting.
If the direction
\mathbin{\prec:}
were to hold in the law for the refined codomain, it would not only imply the existence of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}
from
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
but also allow using
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}
as a representative of the equivalence class of possible values for
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}
.
That is in keeping with our goal of avoiding changes of representation. Therefore:

Definition 2
(Quotiented Codomain)

We adopt the rule below (which is an equality with Thm.
6.2
) as an axiom whenever either side is well-formed:

\displaystyle\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\mathbin{\prec:}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B})$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f,g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\right)$}}}}

(**)

Aggregating the above laws, we obtain a normalization algorithm for types:

Theorem 6.3
(Normalizing Types)

Every type is equal to a type of the form
\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt)$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}
where
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A},\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}::={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0,1}t}^{*}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}.~\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
In particular, if a type does not use refined domains, it is equal to a quotient of a refinement of a DHOL type.

Proof

Using Thm.
6.2
with the axiom from Def.
2
, all refinements and quotients can be pushed out of all function types except for a single refinement of the domain; if there is no such refinement, we can use
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}:=\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}
.
And using Thm.
6.1
, those can be collected into a single quotient+refinement.

Together with the equality and variance rules from Thm.
3.2
,
4.1
,
5.1
, this induces a
subtype-checking algorithm
that reduces subtyping to validity without ever expanding Def.
1
.
The latter is important because expanding Def.
1
would recurse into a computationally expensive problem.
This algorithm is obviously sound as it only chains derived rules.
We are confident, but have not proved yet, that it is also complete in the sense that subtyping can always be derived by using only our derived rules (i.e., without Def.
1
) and a sound and complete theorem prover (which we obtain in Sect.
7
).

It may be surprising and certainly complicates subtype-checking that we need to allow for refined domains in the normal forms.
This limitation echoes an observation first made in
[
13
]
about combining dependent types and refinements.
Effectively, the culprits are partial dependent functions that cannot be extended to total functions because the return type is well-defined and non-empty only for the refined domain.
For future work, it would be interesting to use a higher-order logic with partial functions as a translation target, like the one of
[
9
]
.
But we have not considered that option due to the lack of ATP support for such logics.

7
Soundness and Completeness

We extend the translation from Fig.
3
with cases for our two new productions:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\left(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\right)}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}:={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\land\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\land\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\\[1.99997pt]

These definitions are not surprising as PERs in HOL are known to be closed under refinements and quotients.

These are indeed not equivalent in line with our observation from Ex.
5
.

Like in
[
21
]
, this translation yields a sound (defined as in the previous paper) and complete theorem prover:

Theorem 7.1
(Completeness)

We have the invariants from Fig.
4
.

if in DHOL

then in HOL

\phantom{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}\ \mathsf{Thy}

\phantom{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \mathsf{Thy}

\phantom{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\ \mathsf{Ctx}

\phantom{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \mathsf{Ctx}

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}
and

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}:\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}
is a PER

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbin{\equiv}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}},\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Figure 4
:
Invariants of the Translation

Proof

The subtyping claim is a slightly strengthened version of the claim obtained by expanding the definition of
\mathbin{\prec:}
.
The proof, given in Appendix
0.C
, adapts the proof from
[
21
]
with additional cases for new productions and rules.

As in
[
21
]
, the converse of Thm.
7.1
is much harder to state and prove.
First we need a technical assumption: We call a type symbol
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{a}}

inhabited
if at least one of its instances is provably non-empty.

Theorem 7.2
(Soundness)

In a well-formed DHOL-theory
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}\ \mathsf{Thy}
in which every type symbol is inhabited:

\displaystyle\text{If}\;\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash^{DHOL}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}\;\;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{and}\;\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}}\vdash^{HOL}_{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\;\;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{then}\;\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash^{DHOL}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}

Proof

The key idea is to transform a HOL-proof of
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
into one that is in the image of the translation, at which point we can read off a DHOL-proof of
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
.
The full proof is given in Appendix
0.D
.
We expect the inhabitation requirement to be redundant, but have not been able to complete the proof without it yet.
In any case, it is harmless because it is satisfied by all practical examples.

It is now straightforward to extend the DHOL implementation we gave in
[
21
]
:
First run a bidirectional type-checker for DHOL, using the subtyping-checker sketched in Sect.
6
, to establish well-typedness of theory and conjecture.
Then translate conjecture and generated proof obligations and apply a HOL ATP.

8
Application to Typed Set Theory

DHOL with subtyping enables a novel formalization of typed set-theory:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\mathord{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}},\hskip 14.22636pt\in\mathord{:}\,\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}},\hskip 14.22636pt\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{elem}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}:=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}|_{\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}}\hskip 1.13809pt

The key idea is that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{elem}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}
is the DHOL
type
of set-theoretical elements of the
set

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}
.
Leveraging that refinements and quotients do not require change of representation, we obtain a powerful combination of elegant high-level typed formalization and efficient low-level reasoning.
All the routine constructions of untyped set theory can be lifted to their typed counterparts.
For example, for products, we use
\times\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{pair}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
and the property
\triangleright\,\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x,y,s,t}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s})\wedge({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t})\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{pair}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\times\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
, from which we can show that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{pair}\;\mathord{:}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{elem}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{elem}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{elem}\ (s\times t)
.

We can also use DHOL-functions
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}
as set-theoretical functions between sets
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
without a change in representation as the type

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}:=\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}})|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}})|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}})|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}})|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}

where

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
and

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}\,(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})
.
This allows us to represent set-theoretical function application and composition
\circ
directly as DHOL application/composition.

Consequently, theorem proving in typed set theory becomes very strong because a large share of the proving workload can be outsourced into typing-obligations.
For example, the property that the composition of functions
f:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
and
g:\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}
has type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}
becomes

\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s,t,u}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{Functions}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g}\ \circ\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f})\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}

which yields in HOL the conjecture below that current HOL ATPs solve easily.

\displaystyle\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow((\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}))\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u})

Below is the HOL translation of the conjecture that function composition is associative. It is similarly easily proved by current ATPs:

\displaystyle\triangleright\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{v}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{v}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{u}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{h}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{h}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~x\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{h}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{v})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathrm{set}}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{x}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{x}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\Rightarrow(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{set\_rel}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{h}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x})))\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{h}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{g}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{f}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}))))

The corresponding TPTP files are available at

https://gl.mathhub.info/MMT/LATIN2/-/tree/master/source/casestudies/2025-FroCos
.

9
Conclusion and Future Work

DHOL combines higher-order logic with dependent types, obtaining an intuitive and expressive language, albeit with undecidable typing.
We double down on this design by elegantly extending DHOL with two practically important type constructors that thrive in that setting: refinement and quotient types.
Like dependent function types, these two require terms occurring in types.
Both are near-impossible to add as an afterthought to a type theory with decidable typing.

We translate the resulting logic to HOL, obtaining a practical automated theorem proving workflow for DHOL with refinement and quotient types.
Our main result is the proof of soundness and completeness of this translation.

We used an extensional subtyping approach, where
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\mathbin{\prec:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
holds iff all
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
-terms also have type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{B}
.
This allows combining typed representations and efficient reasoning.
We established all the expected variance and normalization laws except for function types with refined domains.
Future work must investigate how to improve on this to make normalizing types and thus subtype-checking simpler.

We also want to carry our results for DHOL over to existing refinement/quotient type systems for programming languages like Quotient Haskell, where DHOL-like axioms are used as lightweight specifications.

Appendix 0.A
Summary of logics and translations

In this section we collect the inference rules of the logics and the definition of the overall translation.
We name the rules and enumerate the cases in the definition of the translation for reference in the proofs in the subsequent appendices.

Rules for implication:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}
(whose existence follows from rule (
)), so we denote this term by

Figure 5:
HOL Rules

0.A.2
Derived rules

Using the rules given in Figure
5
we can derive a number of additional useful rules.

The following lemma collects a few routine meta-theorems that we make use of later on:

Lemma 4

Given the inference rules for HOL (cfg. Figure
5
), the following rules are admissible:

\cfrac{\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\ \mathsf{Ctx}\hypertarget{ctxThy}{}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\vdash\,}}T\ \mathsf{Thy}}\,\text{ctxThy}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}\hypertarget{tpCtx}{}}{\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\ \mathsf{Ctx}}\,\text{tpCtx}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\hypertarget{typingTp}{}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{tp}}}\,\text{typingTp}\hskip 14.22636pt\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\hypertarget{validTyping}{}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\mathord{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\text{validTyping}\hskip 14.22636pt

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\forall}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}:=\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{=}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{bool}}}\,\lambda{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}}

\cfrac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt\hypertarget{psubE}{}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma}\mathbin{\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}T}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}}\,\text{$|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{p}}\hskip 1.13809pt$E2}

We also have the axiom from Definition
2
.

Finally, we modify the rule (
) for the non-emptiness of types: we allow the existence of empty dependent types and only require that for each HOL type in the image of the translation there exists one non-empty DHOL type translated to it (rather than requiring all dependent types translated to it to be non-empty).
Observe that either restricting to the fragment HOL of DHOL or translating to it then yields the non-emptyness assumptions for HOL types.

Appendix 0.B
The translation from DHOL into HOL

Before actually going into the soundness and completeness proofs, we repeat and enumerate the cases in the definition of the translation, so we can reference them in the following.

Definition 3
(Translation)

We define a translation from DHOL to HOL syntax by induction on the Grammar.

We use the notation
\overrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}},\overrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}.~},\overrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}
and
\overrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}

to denote
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}\mathord{:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{n}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{1}}}.~\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\Pi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{\mathord{:}}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{n}}}.~
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{1}}\to\ldots\to\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A_{n}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{1}}\ \ldots\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{n}}

respectively.

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle:=\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(PT16)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\left(\nicefrac{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{A}$}}}}{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{r}$}}}}\right)}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}*}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{s}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{t}
